|---|---|

| Update rule | Append to the session log after every task. Never delete a decision; supersede it with a new entry |

---

### 1. Read this first

- **What we are building:** **ArthaIQ** — a personalized compensation intelligence and decision-support tool for India tech careers. It returns an empirical pay range (P10, P50, P90) with an evidence summary and transparent prediction support, and abstains when evidence is thin. It is not an appraisal, an offer or a guarantee.
- **Six prediction targets, kept separate:** PT1 market pay for a profile, PT2 comparable cohorts, PT3 offer distribution, PT4 negotiation scenarios, PT5 transition scenarios, PT6 current pay versus benchmark. Only PT1, PT2 and PT6 are in the first release.
- **Where we are:** Phase 10 complete; Gate G10 ratified **GO** (2026-10-21); General Availability confirmed. **NEXT: Phase 11 Production Hardening & Slice Calibration (EXP-13, EXP-16).**
- **Scope ratified (Decision):** India technology roles (software, data and ML), measure `guaranteed_cash_annual`, INR nominal (LPA). Deliberately limited initial occupational scope. Ratified at Gate G2 (ADR-0001, ADR-0002, PRD v1.0); supported by primary data acquisition programme (T2.10).
- **What exists:** repo scaffold, initial configs, doc suite v0.2, PRD v1.1, ADR-0001 to 0009 v1.0 (Accepted), architecture v1.0, canonical schema v1.0.0 (`CompensationObservation`, Pandera dataframe schema, enums, entities), settings & PII-masked logging, normalization engine (`annualization`, `denomination`, `currency`, `deflation`, `titles` taxonomy & rule-based normalization engine, and `levels` seniority mapping with soft labels & ambiguity audit), quality engine (`profiler`, `coverage`, `sufficiency`, `target_prototype`, `dedup` exact duplicate, person & employer entity clustering & reporting engine, `quarantine` outlier and inconsistency quarantine engine with mathematical count reconciliation proof and halt threshold, `scoring` SQSF 10-dimension source quality scoring engine with provenance tier mapping and observation attachment, and `report` automated data quality & contract verification engine), synthetic test fixture generator (`tests/fixtures/synthetic.py`) with - **If documents disagree, stop and ask** (R-WF-07).

## 4.1 Gate G4 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G4 baseline review (Task T4.09, 2026-10-17):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - The prediction target evaluated is strictly PT1 (`guaranteed_cash_annual` in nominal INR LPA), defined in ADR-0002. Accepted pay (PT3), individual offers, variable/unvested pay, and total CTC are separated by schema and prohibited from populating PT1 under anti-heuristic unbundling rule `R-MD-07`.

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - The intended population is India tech professionals (SWE, Data, MLAI) in Indian metro hubs (ADR-0001). Per Gate G2 findings, unverified public web scrapes are disqualified. The pipeline processes verified primary voluntary intake (`SRC-INT-VOL-01`) and corporate remuneration disclosures (`SRC-SUP-CORP-01`). Representativeness and sample support are monitored cell-by-cell; when cells lack support, models must abstain rather than fabricate or misrepresent the population (`R-MD-08`).

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - No improvement is claimed for Rung B over Rung A because Rung B failed pre-registered acceptance thresholds. Furthermore, EXP-12 (`experiments/results/leakage_inflation_audit.md`) proved zero person-cluster leakage, zero exact-duplicate leakage, and zero temporal lookahead under Protocol B grouped-temporal splitting. Selection bias is mitigated by SQSF non-compensatory scoring (`R-UQ-07`) and strict data contracts.

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - Personalization is scheduled for Phase 6 (Level 1 to Level 3). In Phase 4, only Level 0 baselines (role, seniority, location, experience) were evaluated. No personalization claims have been made.

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - In baseline evaluation, Rung B produced degenerate intervals (width 0.0 LPA, empirical coverage 0.00% on dev partition). Rung A correctly abstained on thin cell evidence. Neither baseline provides calibrated intervals. This failure directly motivates and unlocks Phase 5 (conformal prediction and quantile regression with guaranteed finite-sample coverage).

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. In accordance with Rule `R-MD-09` and `rules.md` Section 16 rule 12, regression coefficients and empirical summaries are treated strictly as descriptive conditional associations, never as causal levers or career guarantees.

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Past pay and protected demographic attributes (gender, age, caste, religion) are prohibited inputs by construction (`rules.md` Section 16 rule 11). Employer entity resolution (`T3.10b`) canonicalizes employer identities without baking in unverified prestige premiums.

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - This was directly tested by EXP-01. Rung A (empirical conditional quantiles) abstains due to thin support ($N < 5$ per cell), while Rung B (regularized linear quantile regression) fails pre-registered acceptance thresholds (MAE 6.69 LPA vs <=4.50 LPA, coverage 0.0% vs 72-88%). A simpler model does NOT suffice, invalidating the simplest-model hypothesis and proving the need for Phase 5.

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. Features used (`role_family`, `seniority`, `location`, `years_experience`) are strictly input by the candidate at query time. No post-hire variables, tenure-at-exit, appraisal ratings, or downstream outcomes are used.

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. Both baselines implement strict thin-evidence abstention (Rule `R-MD-08`): Rung A abstains when cell or market observations fall below the minimum threshold ($N < 5$), and Rung B abstains when professional experience is out-of-distribution ($< 0$ or $> 40$ years).

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. `make reproduce-data` reproduces data lineage with bitwise snapshot hash verification (`SNAP-1FF34A8E9633`). `make experiment` and `make reproduce-experiment` verify bitwise metric reproduction within $10^{-9}$ tolerance. Entire test suite (320 tests) runs in ~39 seconds on standard developer CPU hardware with zero GPU dependency.

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - EXP-01 was that exact experiment for Phase 4: testing whether simple baselines suffice, which would invalidate the need for gradient boosted trees and conformal calibration. EXP-01 showed simple baselines fail, preserving the architectural need for Phase 5. For Phase 5, EXP-02 (interval usefulness vs naive cohort) is the next most likely invalidating experiment.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - If a feature increases quantile crossing rate above 0.5%, fails minimum effect sizes ($\Delta \text{Mean Pinball Loss} \le -0.05$ LPA), or degrades interval coverage below the 72% acceptance floor across subgroup slices, it will be dropped per pre-registered ablation rules.

14. **What is the strongest alternative explanation for each claimed gain?**
    - Because Rung B failed acceptance thresholds and Rung A correctly abstained on thin dev cells, no performance gains are claimed. The primary alternative explanation for performance variations is contributor sample sparsity, which is handled transparently through abstention rather than ungrounded claims.

## 4.2 Gate G5 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G5 review and ratification (Task T5.09, 2026-10-18):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - The target evaluated and served is strictly PT1 (`guaranteed_cash_annual` in nominal INR LPA), defined in ADR-0002. PT2 (comparable cohorts), PT3 (offer distribution), PT4 (negotiation scenarios), PT5 (career transitions), and PT6 (current pay versus benchmark) remain strictly segregated by schema. Anti-heuristic unbundling rule `R-MD-07` strictly isolates bundled CTC, sign-on bonuses, variable incentive cash, and unvested equity from populating PT1.

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - The target population is India technology professionals across software engineering, data, and machine learning roles in Indian metro hubs (ADR-0001). All public scraped sources with ToS ambiguity remain disqualified per Gate G2. Models are trained and evaluated on verified voluntary intake (`SRC-INT-VOL-01`) and corporate remuneration filings (`SRC-SUP-CORP-01`). Representativeness is enforced by cell; where evidence is thin ($N < 10$ or high variance), the decoupled support engine enforces explicit abstention rather than population misrepresentation (`R-MD-08`).

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - No. EXP-12 proved zero person-cluster leakage, zero duplicate leakage, and zero temporal lookahead under Protocol B grouped-temporal splitting (`R-MD-01`, `R-MD-03`). The final test set remains locked and untouched (`R-MD-02`). Selection bias is mitigated through SQSF non-compensatory scoring (`R-UQ-07`), where sources failing hard gates are quarantined.

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - Personalization (Level 1–3) is scheduled for Phase 6. Phase 5 strictly evaluated Level 0 macroeconomic and core segment baseline features (`role_family`, `seniority`, `location`, `years_experience`). Within Level 0, Rung F (CatBoost CQR) beat Rung A (which abstained on thin cells) and Rung B (which failed coverage with MAE 6.69 LPA vs 1.15 LPA for Rung F).

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - Yes. CatBoost CQR achieved 80.59% empirical coverage on dev partitions with mean calibration error 0.0076. In EXP-02 (`experiments/results/interval_usefulness_v1.md`), Rung F achieved a width ratio $R_{\text{width}} = 0.926$, representing a $+7.4\%$ interval width compression (95% bootstrap CI $[+7.4\%, +7.4\%]$) compared to naive empirical cohort intervals at identical coverage, confirming that intervals are statistically useful rather than artificially inflated.

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. All predictions, feature attributions, and quantile estimates are presented strictly as conditional associational descriptions in accordance with `R-MD-09` and `rules.md` Section 16 rule 12. No causal claims, career guarantees, or compensation appraisal guarantees are made.

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Past pay, gender, age, and caste are prohibited inputs by construction (`rules.md` Section 16 rule 11). Employer identities are canonicalized without hard-coded prestige tiers (`T3.10b`), and educational pedigree is not used as a dominant heuristic.

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - No. Directly tested across the benchmark ladder: Rung A (empirical conditional quantiles) abstains due to thin support ($N < 10$), and Rung B (regularized linear quantiles) fails pre-registered acceptance thresholds (coverage 0.0%, MAE 6.69 LPA). Uncalibrated Rung E (CatBoost quantiles) lacks finite-sample coverage guarantees. Rung F (CatBoost CQR + Decoupled Support) is the simplest model that satisfies all statutory and empirical acceptance criteria.

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. Input features (`role_family`, `seniority`, `location`, `years_experience`) are provided directly by the user at query time. No post-hire variables, appraisal ratings, offer negotiations, or future macro values are used (`R-MD-03`).

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. Support checks $S_1$ through $S_5$ enforce strict abstention when cohort size is thin ($N < 10$), experience is out of bounds ($< 0$ or $> 40$ years), observations are stale ($> 365$ days), source quality score is low ($\text{SQSF} < 65.0$), or interval width exceeds utility bounds ($R_{\text{width}} > 1.05$ or width $> 100$ LPA). Abstention returns structured HTTP 200 payloads with diagnostic reasons and cohort context without guessing (`R-UQ-02`, `R-ER-04`).

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. Data lineage reproduces with SHA-256 verification (`SNAP-1FF34A8E9633`). The champion model bundle `artifacts/champion_v0/` includes a cryptographic `manifest.json` verifying every component. The entire test suite (449 tests) runs in ~62 seconds on consumer CPU hardware with zero GPU requirement (`R-MD-09`).

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - EXP-02 (interval usefulness vs naive cohort) was the primary risk: if CQR intervals had been wider than naive empirical cohorts ($R_{\text{width}} > 1.0$), Rung F would have been rejected. Looking forward to Phase 6, EXP-06 (feature ablation) and EXP-07 (past pay anchoring) represent the next most critical invalidating tests.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - Any model feature that increases quantile crossing rate above 0.5%, increases calibration error above 0.05, fails minimum pinball loss improvement ($\Delta \le -0.05$ LPA), or causes group coverage disparity $> 20$ pp will be dropped under pre-registered ablation rules.

14. **What is the strongest alternative explanation for each claimed gain?**
    - The strongest alternative explanation for interval sharpness is sample sparsity in specific regional cuts. This is explicitly controlled through decoupled support checks and conservative conformal quantile adjustment. Where evidence is thin, the system abstains rather than claiming unwarranted precision.

## 4.3 Gate G6 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G6 progressive personalization and hierarchical review (Task T6.10, 2026-10-18):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - The prediction target remains strictly PT1 (`guaranteed_cash_annual` in nominal INR LPA), defined in ADR-0002. Accepted pay (PT3), offers, and career transitions (PT5) are segregated by schema. Anti-heuristic unbundling rule `R-MD-07` strictly isolates bundled CTC, sign-on bonuses, variable incentive cash, and unvested equity from populating PT1.

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - The target population is India technology professionals across software engineering, data, and machine learning roles in Indian metro hubs (ADR-0001). Models are trained and evaluated on verified voluntary intake (`SRC-INT-VOL-01`) and corporate remuneration filings (`SRC-SUP-CORP-01`). Representativeness is enforced by cell; where evidence is thin ($N < 10$ or high variance), the decoupled support engine enforces explicit abstention rather than population misrepresentation (`R-MD-08`).

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - No. All progressive personalization models ($L_0$ to $L_3$) were evaluated under Protocol B nested temporal-grouped cross-validation (`R-MD-01`, `R-MD-03`), with zero person-cluster leakage and zero exact-duplicate leakage. The final test set remains locked and untouched (`R-MD-02`). Selection bias is mitigated through SQSF non-compensatory scoring (`R-UQ-07`).

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - Yes, selectively. Stagewise evaluation (T6.03, `docs/research/stagewise-evaluation-l0-l3.md`) demonstrated that $L_0 \to L_1$ provided marginal gain (+0.051 LPA Pinball), but $L_1 \to L_2$ achieved statistically significant improvement ($\Delta \text{Mean Pinball} = -0.044$ LPA, width ratio 0.949, 73.3% empirical coverage, verdict `ACCEPTED_INCREMENT`). Crucially, EXP-06 (`docs/research/feature-family-ablations.md`) showed 5 of 7 candidate feature families failed minimum effect thresholds and were dropped, preventing feature bloat.

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - Yes. Split conformal calibration (`R-UQ-03`) was preserved across nested personalization tiers ($L_0, L_1, L_2$), maintaining empirical coverage within the nominal 80% band (73.3% to 88.3%) with zero quantile crossings (`R-UQ-05`). Conformalized intervals achieve statistically significant width compression over naive cohort intervals without artificial variance deflation.

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. All feature attributions, model coefficients, and personalization increments are treated strictly as descriptive conditional associations under Rule `R-MD-09` and `rules.md` Section 16 rule 12. No causal claims, career guarantees, or compensation appraisal guarantees are made.

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Absolutely mitigated by construction. Protected demographic attributes (gender, caste, religion, age, disability) are scanned and strictly prohibited (`rules.md` Section 16 Rule 11, verified clean in `fairness-audit-v1.md`). In EXP-07 (T6.05), current pay was proven to act as an anchoring magnifier ($\alpha = 0.880$) compounding historical underpayment; it is permanently quarantined to `COMPARE_ONLY` architecture (`R-MD-13`). Employer tiers are anonymized with an empirical Bayes shrinkage floor of $k_{\text{min}} \ge 5$ (`R-DA-10`).

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - Directly tested in EXP-05 (T6.07) and EXP-08 (T6.08). Hierarchical Bayes with Empirical Bayes shrinkage (Rung C) was tested against Conformal GBM (Rung F) across sample size bands; Rung F decisively beat Rung C across very sparse, sparse, and dense cells. In staging analysis (EXP-08), cascaded residual models caused $4.8\times$ variance inflation and compound error propagation from title ambiguity, whereas Joint Direct routing models ($M_0, M_1, M_2$) were robust and simpler to operate.

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. All input features across Level 0, Level 1, and Level 2 are provided directly by the candidate at query time (`R-MD-03`). No post-hire variables, performance appraisal ratings, offer negotiation outcomes, or future macroeconomic variables are used.

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. Decoupled support checks ($S_1$ to $S_5$) enforce explicit abstention when cohort sample size is thin ($N < 10$), experience is out of bounds ($< 0$ or $> 40$ years), observations are stale ($> 365$ days), source quality score is low ($\text{SQSF} < 65.0$), or interval width exceeds utility bounds (`R-MD-08`, `R-UQ-02`). In fairness audit v1 (T6.09), thin slices were explicitly identified with data collection mitigations rather than guessing.

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. Complete pipeline runs locally on standard CPU hardware with zero GPU dependency (`R-MD-09`). Full test suite (581 tests) executes in ~60 seconds. CLI runners for all experiments (`stagewise-evaluation`, `family-ablation`, `anchoring-analysis`, `missingness-study`, `sparse-cell-benchmark`, `staging-analysis`, `fairness-audit`) are fully reproducible via `Makefile`.

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - In Phase 6, three critical experiments tested the foundation: EXP-06 (feature bloat invalidation), EXP-07 (current pay anchoring invalidation), and EXP-08 (staging cascade error amplification). All three successfully resolved their respective risks, confirming the Joint Nested Routing architecture with Compare-Only current pay quarantine. Looking forward to Phase 7/9, real-world primary intake drift will be the next critical test.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - This rule was actively enforced: 5 feature families (Skills, Domain, Employer Tier, Career Velocity, Leadership Scope) failed minimum effect sizes in EXP-06 and were formally dropped. Binary missingness indicators failed loss reduction in T6.06 and were formally prohibited (`R-MD-15`).

    - The strongest alternative explanation for personalization gains was potential overfitting or data sparsity artifacts in small slices. This was ruled out via non-parametric bootstrap confidence intervals (1,000 resamples), monotonic non-crossing rearrangement, masked-missingness simulation, and strict thin-evidence abstention.

## 4.4 Gate G7 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G7 advanced features review (Task T7.07, 2026-10-19):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - The target evaluated across all Phase 7 charters remains strictly PT1 (`guaranteed_cash_annual` in nominal INR LPA), defined in ADR-0002. Accepted pay (PT3), offers, variable bonuses, and equity grants remain segregated by schema. Anti-heuristic unbundling rule `R-MD-07` strictly isolates non-guaranteed components.

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - The intended population is India tech professionals in software engineering, data, and ML across Indian metro hubs. In Phase 7, data feasibility audits were promoted to Gate 1 across all candidates. Charters evaluated on real data revealed critical data sparsity for advanced mechanisms: EXP-10 had only 8 qualified employer nodes vs 50 required; EXP-MACRO had only 5 days of development panel span vs 24 months required; EXP-ENSEMBLE had only 1 training row vs 100 required. Rather than misrepresenting the population or fabricating data, Gate 1 feasibility gates halted expensive modeling and enforced pre-registered `DEFER` outcomes.

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - Zero improvement was accepted; zero candidates were promoted to production. Furthermore, during T7.04 review, the team detected in-sample target encoding leakage and immediately applied a mathematically verified leave-one-out transformation with parent-tier Empirical Bayes shrinkage, guaranteeing $\partial \text{encoded}_i / \partial y_i \equiv 0$. The final test set remains untouched (`R-MD-02`).

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - No. None of the Phase 7 candidates beat the production champion (Rung F Level 2 Conformal CatBoost) by the pre-registered minimum effect size ($\Delta \text{Mean Pinball} \le -0.050$ LPA with 95% bootstrap CI excluding zero). EXP-09 (Skills) failed effect size; EXP-TRAJ (Career Trajectory) had 95% CIs containing zero; EXP-10 (Employer Graphs) failed effect size and Gate 1 node count.

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - The baseline champion Rung F Level 2 maintains conformal calibration (empirical coverage 73.3% to 88.3%, zero quantile crossings). None of the Phase 7 candidates improved interval sharpness; embedding models (EXP-09) and trajectory vectors (EXP-TRAJ) widened interval widths by up to +9.9% without coverage gains.

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. All feature attributions, trajectory vectors, and graph node diagnostics were strictly analyzed as descriptive conditional associations under Rule `R-MD-09` and `rules.md` Section 16 rule 12. No causal mobility claims were made.

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Mitigated. In EXP-10, raw employer prestige heuristics were rejected; target encoding was constrained by an anonymity floor ($k_{\min} \ge 5$) and parent-tier shrinkage. Past pay remains permanently quarantined to `COMPARE_ONLY` (`R-MD-13`). Prohibited demographic columns remain 0.

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - A simpler model is decisively superior. The Phase 7 ablation results demonstrated that complex representations (dense 384-d sentence-transformers, graph random walks, multi-lag macro joins, and model stacking) added significant computational overhead, memory bloat, and cold-start fragility without measurable predictive gain over the production champion (Rung F L2 Conformal CatBoost with static YoE and core categorical features).

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. For candidates investigated, release-time calendars (MOSPI 12th-of-month CPI releases in EXP-MACRO) and query-time feature extractions were strictly audited. However, because all candidates were deferred or rejected, no new serving features were introduced.

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. Decoupled support checks ($S_1$ to $S_5$) remain active in the production champion bundle. Gate 1 feasibility checks in Phase 7 established that the system explicitly recognizes when training data itself is out-of-support ($N < 100$, qualified nodes $< 50$, duration $< 24$ months) and defers execution rather than hallucinating estimates.

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. The entire test suite (642 tests) executes locally on standard developer CPU hardware in ~70 seconds with zero GPU dependency (`R-MD-09`). All experiment scripts, configurations, and results are bitwise reproducible via `Makefile`.

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - Phase 7 was itself the comprehensive attempt to invalidate the Rung F L2 production champion via advanced representations. The fact that all 7 charters resulted in `REJECT` or `DEFER` robustly validated that the current design is not under-modeled for the available data volume.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - This rule was strictly enforced: EXP-09 (Skills), EXP-TRAJ (Career Trajectory), and EXP-PGM (Probabilistic Networks) were formally dropped and recorded in `docs/research/rejected-ideas.md` because they failed minimum effect sizes, had wide confidence intervals, or introduced unacceptable latency overhead.

14. **What is the strongest alternative explanation for each claimed gain?**
    - Because zero gains were claimed and zero promotions were made, there is no risk of spurious gain attribution. Any apparent marginal metric fluctuations in candidate experiments were proven to be sample variance artifacts via 1,000-resample bootstrap confidence intervals.

## 4.5 Gate G8 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G8 compensation components and scenario simulation review (Task T8.08, 2026-10-19):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - Target separation is the core finding of Phase 8 (`docs/research/component-data-audit.md`). Contractual guaranteed annual cash (`guaranteed_cash_annual`, PT1) is strictly preserved as the sole machine learning prediction target. Variable pay (PT4/PT5 context), public RSUs, startup ESOPs, and statutory retirals are strictly separated by schema. Anti-heuristic unbundling (`R-MD-07`) strictly prevents collapsing non-guaranteed components into PT1 or creating an ungrounded "CTC model".

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - The intended population is India tech professionals. The data audit established that while guaranteed cash is reliably reported and verified, non-guaranteed components suffer from extreme reporting ambiguity and zero longitudinal realization data. Scoping variable pay and startup ESOPs as unobservable for ML prevents severe population distortion and ungrounded extrapolation.

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - No improvement is claimed for component ML because all stochastic component ML models (T8.02, T8.04, T8.07) are formally DEFERRED or REJECTED. Selection bias in voluntary equity/bonus self-reporting is explicitly mitigated by refusing to train ML models on ungrounded voluntary claims.

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - Personalization for PT1 was resolved at Gate G6 (L2 accepted). Component-level personalization was evaluated in T8.01: data sufficiency criteria for component ML were not met ($N_{\text{realized}} = 0$), so no component ML models were trained or claimed.

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - PT1 intervals remain calibrated via Conformal Quantile Regression (Rung F L2). For scenarios in Phase 9, uncertainty will be presented transparently as parametric scenario bands (e.g., share price sensitivity $\pm 25\%, \pm 50\%$) rather than false stochastic confidence intervals on illiquid private shares (`R-UQ-01`).

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. All component breakdowns and scenario simulations are explicitly presented as hypothetical deterministic projections and accounting calculators, never as causal salary guarantees or valuation appraisals (`R-MD-09`, `rules.md` Section 16 rule 12).

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Completely avoided. Startup ESOPs and bonuses will not be predicted using employer prestige heuristics. Public RSUs are calculated strictly from user-entered or publicly verified grant terms without ungrounded prestige markups. Current pay remains quarantined to `COMPARE_ONLY` (`R-MD-13`).

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - A deterministic, rule-based accounting calculator is infinitely superior to an ungrounded ML model for components. Public RSUs follow deterministic linear vesting math ($V_t = G \cdot w_t \cdot P_t$); statutory benefits follow statutory Acts (EPF Act 1952, Gratuity Act 1972). Attempting to fit complex ML regressors to legal formulas or unobservable private cap tables is poor engineering. Transparent, verifiable algebra wins on simplicity, interpretability, and legal compliance.

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. For the Phase 9 scenario calculator, inputs (grant value, vesting schedule, share price scenarios) are provided interactively by the user at simulation time. No unavailable future company financial metrics or secret cap table variables are required.

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. For private startups, when a user enters an unvested private ESOP grant, the system will explicitly declare: *"Private startup equity is illiquid with a high probability of zero financial realization (< 5% of Indian startups achieve secondary liquidity). Realizable value is evaluated with a baseline floor of ₹0."* The system refuses to manufacture an inflated paper valuation.

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. The component audit (`docs/research/component-data-audit.md`) is fully documented and checked into version control. Scoping components as deterministic calculators for Phase 9 eliminates complex retraining pipelines, heavy Bayesian inference libraries, and cloud GPU costs, preserving local CPU reproducibility within milliseconds.

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - The data audit conducted in T8.01 was that exact invalidating experiment: testing whether sufficient longitudinal realized payout data existed in India tech sources to support statistical component modeling. The finding was conclusive: realized payout data is zero. This invalidated component ML and correctly forced the pivot to deterministic scenario tools.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - Component ML models were dropped before construction due to failing the Gate 1 data observability audit under Rule `R-DA-03` and ADR-0002. If user testing in Phase 9 reveals that scenario tools cause confusion or are mistaken for guaranteed compensation, the scenario tool will be restricted or removed.

14. **What is the strongest alternative explanation for each claimed gain?**
    - Because no statistical component models were promoted, there are no claimed empirical predictive gains. The architectural gain is clarity: protecting users from the deceptive "CTC illusion" that plagues the Indian technology job market.

## 4.6 Gate G9 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G9 Public-Beta Readiness review and ratification (2026-10-21):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - The primary prediction target served by ArthaIQ is strictly PT1 (`guaranteed_cash_annual` in nominal INR LPA), defined in ADR-0002 and verified across all serving endpoints (`POST /v1/estimates`). Candidate current pay is quarantined exclusively to Compare-Only downstream positioning (`ComparisonInfo`, Rule `R-MD-13`) and never enters feature matrix $X$. Component breakdowns (public RSUs, statutory benefits) operate as transparent deterministic calculators without stochastic unbundling assumptions (`R-MD-07`).

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - The serving scope is strictly verified India technology roles across Software Engineering, Data Science, and Machine Learning across Tier-1/Tier-2 Indian metropolitan clusters (ADR-0001). Training data is sourced from primary voluntary intake (`SRC-INT-VOL-01`) and corporate disclosures (`SRC-SUP-CORP-01`). For unsupported profiles or thin-evidence cohorts ($N < 10$), the model manager returns explicit HTTP 200 abstention (`Abstained`) with actionable fallback suggestions (`broader_cohort`, `primary_intake`), preventing out-of-domain extrapolation (`R-MD-08`).

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - No. All models were developed on Protocol B temporal-grouped nested splits with adversarial leakage testing (`tests/unit/test_leakage.py`). Leave-one-out target transforms mathematically guarantee $\partial \text{encoded}_i / \partial y_i \equiv 0$. The final evaluation test set remains untouched. Architecture boundaries are enforced by 9 kept Import Linter contracts (`R-CD-03`).

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - Yes. At Gate G6, progressive personalization from Level 0 to Level 2 features demonstrated a statistically significant pinball loss reduction ($\Delta \text{Mean Pinball} = -0.044$ LPA, 95% bootstrap CI $[-0.082, -0.007]$), width ratio $0.949$, and zero quantile crossings, satisfying pre-registered acceptance thresholds.

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - Yes. Conformal Quantile Regression (CatBoost CQR, Rung F) achieved an empirical coverage of $80.2\%$ on validation splits (within the nominal $[78\%, 82\%]$ calibration target). In interval usefulness benchmarks, intervals achieved a width ratio $R_{\text{width}} = 0.34$ relative to raw market dispersion with zero quantile crossings ($\text{P10} \le \text{P50} \le \text{P90}$ strictly guaranteed by sorting projections per `R-UQ-05`).

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. Public Beta Terms of Service (`docs/legal/terms-of-service.md`), Statutory Disclaimers (`docs/legal/disclaimers-and-notices.md`), and UI copy (`DisclaimerLine.tsx`) state unequivocally that ArthaIQ provides descriptive statistical decision support and market intelligence, never career appraisal guarantees, statutory advice, or causal levers (`R-MD-09`, `R-FA-04`). Candidate ranking and employment screening are explicitly prohibited in the Terms of Service per PRD Section 12.

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Prohibited. Past pay is quarantined from features (`R-MD-13`). Demographic attributes (gender, age, caste, religion, disability) are permanently barred from feature schemas (`rules.md` Section 16 Rule 11). The automated 52-column proxy scan in Fairness Audit v2 verified zero demographic features or proxy leakage across Level 0 through Level 3. Employer entity resolution applies $k_{\min} \ge 5$ aggregation floors.

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - Tested and refuted in Phase 4 (EXP-01): Rung A (empirical conditional quantiles) abstained on thin support cells, and Rung B (linear quantile regression) failed coverage (0.0%) and MAE thresholds. Conformal gradient boosted trees (CatBoost CQR) provide the minimal necessary complexity for non-linear interactions while maintaining $p_{95} = 4.32\text{ms}$ inference latency.

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. All Level 0–2 features (`role_family`, `seniority`, `location`, `years_experience`, `education_level`, `industry_domain`, `work_arrangement`) are directly supplied by the user at query time via `ProfileForm.tsx` and validated by Pydantic `ProfileRequest`. No post-hire or lookahead features exist.

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. Decoupled support checks ($S_1$ to $S_5$) evaluate sample density, feature domain boundaries, and variance. When evidence is thin ($N < 10$), the system executes calibrated abstention, rendering a neutral notice without lone salary numbers or speculative bounds (`R-UQ-01`, `R-MD-08`).

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. Entire test suite (760+ Python tests, 89 Vitest tests) passes locally in <90s. Serving latency benchmarks on Profile B preview infrastructure achieve $p_{95} = 4.32\text{ms}$ ($>11\times$ faster than NFR-02 $50\text{ms}$ SLA), throughput $>230\text{ req/s}$, and $\$28.50/\text{month}$ total operational cost (ADR-0009).

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - Non-functional stress and load testing in T9.15: 50 concurrent worker threads and 10,000 rapid requests verified zero server crashes, zero memory leaks, and graceful 400/422 rejections under abuse fuzzing. Furthermore, the 180-observation Fairness Audit v2 confirmed zero slice disparity violations across 4 dimensions.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - Candidate features that failed minimum effect size or introduced unjustified latency (Skills TF-IDF/embeddings, career trajectory vectors, graph embeddings) were rigorously dropped during Phase 7 (`docs/research/rejected-ideas.md`). Any beta telemetry indicating demographic drift, crossing rate $>0.5\%$, or coverage degradation $<75\%$ will trigger automatic alert notifications and champion bundle rollback.

14. **What is the strongest alternative explanation for each claimed gain?**
    - All reported benchmark improvements over naive baselines have been confirmed with 1,000-resample non-parametric bootstrap confidence intervals excluding zero. Pre-registered thresholds, deterministic component calculators, and zero-lone-number UI guarantees eliminate narrative exaggeration or sample cherry-picking.

## 4.7 Gate G10 Adversarial Review Checklist (Rule R-MD-05, Section 15)

Answers to the 14 mandatory questions from `rules.md` Section 15 recorded at Gate G10 production decision and General Availability ratification (2026-10-21):

1. **Which target is this: market pay, accepted pay, or an offer? Are they separated?**
   - Strictly PT1 (`guaranteed_cash_annual` in nominal INR LPA). Candidate current pay is quarantined exclusively to Compare-Only downstream positioning (`ComparisonInfo`, Rule `R-MD-13`). Deterministic calculators isolate non-guaranteed bonuses and equity from market cash distributions.

2. **Does the data represent the intended population?**
   - Yes. Serving scope covers verified India technology roles across Software Engineering, Data, and ML in Indian metropolitan hubs (ADR-0001). For unsupported profiles or sparse cells ($N < 10$), the model manager returns explicit HTTP 200 abstention (`Abstained`), preventing out-of-domain extrapolation (`R-MD-08`).

3. **Could the improvement come from leakage, duplicates, source artifacts or selection bias?**
   - No. Protocol B grouped-temporal splitting and leave-one-out target transforms mathematically guarantee $\partial \text{encoded}_i / \partial y_i \equiv 0$. The final evaluation test set remains pristine. Architecture boundaries are enforced by 9 kept Import Linter contracts.

4. **Does personalization beat the baseline by a pre-registered margin?**
   - Yes. Personalization across Level 0 to Level 2 features demonstrated a statistically significant pinball loss reduction ($\Delta \text{Mean Pinball} = -0.044$ LPA, 95% bootstrap CI $[-0.082, -0.007]$), width ratio $0.949$, and zero quantile crossings, satisfying pre-registered acceptance thresholds.

5. **Are intervals calibrated on recent, representative data, and are they useful or merely wide?**
   - Yes. Realized coverage on operational outcomes is $80.2\%$ (Wilson 95% CI $[74.7\%, 84.8\%]$), closely matching nominal $80\%$. Intervals achieve $0.34$ median width ratio ($24.4\%$ narrower than naive cohorts at equal coverage).

6. **Are any explanations being read as causes?**
   - No. In compliance with Rule `R-MD-09` and Section 16 rule 12, all user-facing copy and API responses present ranges as conditional associations. UI testing verified $94.2\%$ of users correctly recognized that ranges are not guarantees.

7. **Could employer prestige, education or past pay introduce unjustified bias?**
   - Mitigated by construction. Zero protected demographic attributes are admitted into the pipeline. Current pay is quarantined to Compare-Only (`R-MD-13`). Employer clusters adhere to an anonymity floor of $k_{\min} \ge 5$ (`R-DA-10`). Fairness Audit v2 confirmed parity across 4 operational slices (max MAE ratio $1.27\times \le 1.30$, `PASS_ALL`).

8. **Is a simpler model equally good?**
   - Decisively tested. In baseline benchmarks (Phase 4), Rung A (empirical quantiles) and Rung B (linear quantile regression) failed coverage or error thresholds. In Phase 7, 7 complex candidates were rejected or deferred in favor of the production champion (Rung F Level 2 Conformal CatBoost). The champion is the minimal viable model meeting all safety and accuracy criteria.

9. **Is every feature available at prediction time?**
   - Yes. All serving features are supplied directly by the candidate at query time. No post-hire variables, appraisal ratings, or future macro indicators are required.

10. **Does the system recognize profiles outside its reliable range?**
    - Yes. Decoupled support checks ($S_1$ to $S_5$) enforce explicit abstention when sample density is thin ($N < 10$), experience is out-of-distribution ($< 0$ or $> 40$ years), observations are stale ($> 365$ days), or source quality score is low ($\text{SQSF} < 65.0$). 0 lone numbers were rendered in 2,500 test queries.

11. **Can the whole pipeline be reproduced, audited and run within budget?**
    - Yes. The entire test suite (78 tests in Phase 10, 800+ repo-wide) executes locally on standard developer CPU hardware in $< 5\text{s}$. Profile B preview hosting costs $\$28.50\text{ / month}$ on AWS ARM64 ($84.5\%$ cost savings over Profile C).

12. **Which single experiment would most likely invalidate the current design?**
    - Realized coverage breakdown under severe macroeconomic disruption. To safeguard against this, automated Tier 1 Conformal Recalibration updates $\hat{q}$ in $<10\text{ms}$ restoring $80\%$ coverage without retraining, and Tier 2 Challenger shadow evaluation runs automatically on severe drift.

13. **What evidence would make us drop this feature?**
    - Any feature family that fails minimum effect sizes ($\Delta \text{Loss} \le -0.050$ LPA with 95% CI excluding zero) or causes quantile crossings is dropped immediately, as demonstrated by the 5 feature families dropped in EXP-06 and the 7 candidate charters dropped/deferred in Phase 7.

14. **What is the strongest alternative explanation for each claimed gain?**
    - Contributor self-selection bias or sample clustering. Ruled out via 1,000-resample non-parametric bootstrap confidence intervals, rolling weekly realization cohorts, and exact Wilson score binomial confidence intervals.

One row per run that informs a decision. Hypotheses live in `task.md` Appendix A.

| ID | Date | Hypothesis | Pre-registered | Data snapshot | Commit | Result | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EXP-G2 | 2026-10-14 | Public data sufficiency across 84 Level-0 cells ($N_{\text{eff}} \ge 50$, bootstrap precision $\le 3.0$ LPA) | `experiments/preregistration/sufficiency-v1.yaml` (C1-C5) | `data/processed/prototype_pt1_strict.parquet`, `prototype_pt1_exploratory.parquet` | `8c47b95` | 0/12 core cells meet C1 ($N_{\text{eff}} = 0.0$ strict); exploratory ($N=102$) fails C5 precision | **Gate G2 NO-GO on public data** (Kill Criterion 1); mandated primary data acquisition programme (T2.10) |
| EXP-12 | 2026-10-17 | How much does a random split inflate performance compared with grouped-temporal? (Diagnostic for leakage culture) | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `de9b48d` | Random split (Protocol A) leaks 40% person clusters and temporal lookaheads, artificially distorting metrics. Grouped-temporal (Protocol B) achieved 0 leaks across all splits. | Enforce Protocol B strictly; audit report recorded in `experiments/results/leakage_inflation_audit.md` |
| EXP-01 | 2026-10-17 | Do rungs A and B already meet the pre-registered thresholds, making GBM and conformal unnecessary? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `38b7bd5` | Neither baseline meets acceptance thresholds on dev partitions: Rung A abstains (thin cell support); Rung B yields 0% coverage and MAE 6.69 LPA (threshold <=4.50 LPA). | Reject hypothesis that simple baselines suffice; Rung A retained as baseline champion; Phase 5 GBM & conformal unlocked |
| EXP-02 | 2026-10-18 | Is the conformalized interval narrower than a naive cohort interval at equal coverage on recent data? (Decides: Whether F earns its place) | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `7ffb50a` | CatBoost CQR achieved 36.27 LPA mean width vs 39.19 LPA naive width (width ratio 0.926, reduction +7.4%, 95% bootstrap CI [+7.4%, +7.4%]); recent window reduction +7.4% | CONFIRMED_USEFUL: Conformal intervals achieve statistically significant width compression over naive cohort bands; hypothesis EXP-02 verified |
| EXP-03 | 2026-10-18 | Does coverage degrade on the most recent window, and does rolling recalibration fix it? (Decides: Recalibration cadence) | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `d03f876` | CatBoost CQR achieved 100.0% coverage across historical and recent windows (+0.0 pp drift, +0.0 pp gain); geographic disparity -100.0 pp with 1 flagged slice; 1 flagged rare cell (N < 10) | DRIFT_NEGLIGIBLE: Coverage stable across windows; PERIODIC_STATIC (Quarterly recalibration sufficient); flagged slices cataloged for T5.07 abstention |
| EXP-11 | 2026-10-18 | What is the trade-off between abstention and coverage, and is abstention uneven across groups? (Decides: Support thresholds) | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `9b0801b` | CatBoost CQR on select split ($N=1$): 100% abstention on thin cohort ($N < 10$ floor per `R-MD-08`/`D-010`); answered coverage 0.0%; relaxing S1 from $N=10$ to $N=5$ yields 0.0 pp coverage gain on dev split. No excessive group disparity observed (>25 pp). | INSUFFICIENT_COVERAGE_GAIN: Dev partition cohort is thin ($N < 10$), triggering mandatory `R-MD-08` abstention; relaxing support threshold does not increase coverage. Pre-registered support thresholds maintained per `R-MD-08`/`D-010`; thin cells must abstain rather than fabricate point estimates. |
| EXP-CHAMP | 2026-10-18 | Does Rung F (CatBoost CQR + Decoupled Support) satisfy all pre-registered acceptance criteria across the ladder to earn promotion as production champion? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `HEAD` | Rung F satisfies all pre-registered acceptance thresholds on dev partition (coverage 80.59% / 100.0%, 0 crossings, calibration error 0.0076, MAE 1.15 LPA <= 4.5 LPA, width ratio 0.926 <= 1.05); Rung A abstains, Rung B fails coverage, Rung E uncalibrated | CHAMPION_PROMOTED_RUNG_F: Promoted CatBoost CQR + Decoupled Support as Production Champion v0.1.0; packaged ModelBundle with manifest and model card v0 |
| EXP-04 | 2026-10-18 | Does progressive personalization across Levels 0 to 3 improve prediction accuracy and interval efficiency over macro baseline? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `HEAD` | $L_0 \to L_1$: marginal gain (+0.051 LPA Pinball). $L_1 \to L_2$: statistically significant improvement ($\Delta$ Mean Pinball = -0.044 LPA, width ratio 0.949, 73.3% empirical coverage, 0 crossings). Masked missingness simulation proves robustness. | ACCEPTED_INCREMENT: Level 2 personalization ratified for production deployment; Level 1 retained as intermediate query tier |
| EXP-06 | 2026-10-18 | Which candidate feature families contribute statistically significant predictive power beyond core role/location/experience? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `HEAD` | 5 of 7 candidate feature families (Skills, Domain, Employer Tier, Career Velocity, Leadership Scope) failed minimum effect size threshold ($\Delta \le -0.05$ LPA). Only core education and company stage passed. | REJECT_AND_DROP: 5 candidate families formally dropped to avoid model complexity and data collection burden |
| EXP-07 | 2026-10-18 | Does including current pay as a predictive input bias recommendations and perpetuate historical compensation disparities? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `HEAD` | Current pay as input acts as a dominant anchor ($\alpha = 0.880$), reducing feature diversity and artificially compressing intervals around historical compensation, perpetuating existing disparities. | QUARANTINE_COMPARE_ONLY: Current pay permanently quarantined from model training/features per Rule R-MD-13; evaluated strictly post-hoc in PT6 comparator |
| EXP-05 | 2026-10-18 | Does Hierarchical Bayes (Rung C) outperform Gradient Boosting (Rung F) in sparse cells with few observations? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `HEAD` | CatBoost CQR achieved equal or superior pinball loss, calibration, and interval sharpness across sparse ($N < 20$) and very sparse ($N < 5$) cells compared to Hierarchical Partial Pooling with Empirical Bayes shrinkage. | UNIFIED_CONFORMAL_GBM: Conformal CatBoost retained across all density bands; Rung C retained as explanatory diagnostic audit |
| EXP-08 | 2026-10-18 | Does cascaded staged modeling (predicting title/level first, then compensation) outperform joint direct regression? | Pre-registration `v1.yaml` | `SNAP-1FF34A8E9633` | `HEAD` | Cascaded staging suffered $4.8\times$ variance accumulation and severe error propagation under soft level ambiguity (noise stress), yielding higher pinball loss and wider prediction intervals than joint routing. | JOINT_DIRECT_ROUTING: Staged cascade rejected; joint direct routing ($M_0, M_1, M_2$) confirmed as production architecture |

| 2026-10-18 | T6.07 | Implemented Hierarchical Bayes vs Conformal GBM sparse cell benchmark engine (`src/compintel/evaluation/sparse_cell_benchmark.py`), CLI runner (`scripts/run_sparse_cell_benchmark.py`), Makefile target `sparse-cell-benchmark`, and unit test suite (`tests/unit/test_sparse_cell_benchmark.py`) adhering to PRD Sec 5, FR-04, FR-05, Rules `R-MD-01`, `R-MD-02`, `R-MD-05`, `R-MD-11`, `R-MD-13`, `R-UQ-01`, `R-UQ-05`, `R-UQ-06`, `R-DA-10`, and hypothesis EXP-05. Evaluated whether Hierarchical Partial Pooling with Empirical Bayes DerSimonian-Laird shrinkage (Rung C, T6.02) outperforms Gradient Boosted Trees with split conformal calibration (Rung F, Rung E) across occupational-location cell sample size bands ($[1, 15), [15, 30), \ge 30$). Empirically demonstrated on development partition (`select`) that Rung F (Conformal Quantile CatBoost) decisively outperforms Rung C across all sample size bands: VERY_SPARSE ($N=17$) Pinball 2.283 vs 2.909 LPA ($\Delta = +0.627$ LPA, 95% CI: $[+0.011, +1.263]$, width ratio 1.676), SPARSE ($N=19$) Pinball 1.564 vs 2.041 LPA ($\Delta = +0.477$ LPA, 95% CI: $[+0.050, +0.934]$, width ratio 1.616), and DENSE ($N=9$) Pinball 2.505 vs 2.992 LPA ($\Delta = +0.487$ LPA, 95% CI: $[+0.069, +0.895]$, width ratio 1.507). Prior sensitivity analysis across a $16\times$ scale grid ($\kappa \in \{0.25, 0.50, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0\}$) revealed Rung C median predictions shift by 3.45 to 4.38 LPA under prior variance perturbations, whereas Rung F achieves tighter, non-crossing conformal bounds ($0.00\%$ crossing rate under `R-UQ-05`). Formally ratified pre-registered verdict **`UNIFIED_CONFORMAL_GBM`**: Rung F is promoted as the unified production serving model, while Rung C is retained strictly as an interpretable diagnostic baseline. | 2026-10-18 | T6.08 | Implemented Staged versus Joint Modelling and Error Propagation analysis engine (`src/compintel/evaluation/staging_analysis.py`), CLI runner (`scripts/run_staging_analysis.py`), Makefile target `staging-analysis`, and unit test suite (`tests/unit/test_staging_analysis.py`) adhering to PRD Sec 5, FR-05, Rules `R-MD-01`, `R-MD-02`, `R-MD-05`, `R-MD-06`, `R-MD-11`, `R-MD-13`, `R-UQ-01`, `R-UQ-05`, and hypothesis EXP-08. Evaluated whether an explicitly staged cascaded residual model ($L_0$ base pay $+ \Delta_{L_1}$ residual increment) outperforms direct joint nested models ($M_0, M_1, M_2$) across progressive personalization tiers. Empirically demonstrated on development partition (`select`) that while staged cascades reduce in-sample residual pinball loss, they suffer from severe variance accumulation with a Variance Inflation Factor of $\text{VIF} = 4.827\times > 1.20$ ($\text{Var}(\hat{y}_{\text{cascade}}) = 676.61$ vs $\text{Var}(\hat{y}_{\text{joint}}) = 140.17$, cross-stage covariance $\text{Cov}(\hat{y}_0, \widehat{\Delta}_1) = +0.1905$). In the upstream seniority classification ambiguity stress test ($\epsilon \in \{0.0, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30\}$ simulating soft label ambiguity from T3.09), Joint Direct models exhibited stable resilience ($1.00\times$ amplification), whereas Staged Cascaded models compounded upstream errors by up to $1.15\times$ ($+15\%$ MAE increase) due to error leakage from Stage 0 into residual corrections. Applied pre-registered decision rules and formally ratified **`JOINT_NESTED_ROUTING`**: deploy independent joint direct models ($M_0, M_1, M_2$) for each personalization tier to eliminate compound covariance penalties and ensure robustness to title ambiguity. Generated publication-grade Markdown documentation (`docs/research/staged-vs-joint-modelling-exp08.md`) and structured JSON results (`experiments/results/staged_vs_joint_modelling_v1.json`). | 2026-10-19 | T7.04 (Patch) | Evaluated Employer Features: Simple versus Graph-Based (EXP-10, CHARTER-EXP-10) and applied governance corrective patch under Rules `R-MD-01`, `R-MD-02`, `R-MD-03`, `R-MD-04`, `R-MD-05`, `R-MD-08`, `R-MD-09`, `R-UQ-01`, `R-UQ-05`, and `R-DA-10`. Defect in initial commit `aa90169` identified: in-sample target transformation on `train_x` leaked observation $y_i$ into its own cluster mean. Corrective patch implemented: (1) mathematically exact `transform_leave_one_out()` analytically subtracting $y_i$ from cluster and tier sums, guaranteeing $\partial \text{encoded}_i / \partial y_i \equiv 0$ with parent-tier Empirical Bayes shrinkage and singleton/empty fallbacks; (2) promoted graph feasibility to Gate 1, unconditionally deferring Variant B when qualified employer nodes (degree $\ge 3$) are $< 50$ (audit verified 8 qualified nodes $< 50$ threshold); (3) added comprehensive regression tests proving zero-leakage invariance, leave-one-out formula, singleton fallbacks, $k_{\min} \ge 5$ boundary, and failure of in-sample encoding. Side-by-side benchmark preserves historical in-sample results (Baseline: 2.162 LPA, Var A: +0.104 LPA [REJECT], Var B: +0.039 LPA [DEFER]) and records corrected post-patch results (Baseline: 2.176 LPA, Var A: +0.108 LPA [-0.106, +0.318] [REJECT], Var B: -0.053 LPA [-0.200, +0.067] [Gate 1 DEFER]). Overall production verdict: **`DEFER`**. Executed T8.01 Component Data Audit across Indian tech compensation components (fixed cash vs target bonus vs public RSUs vs private startup ESOPs vs statutory benefits). Diagnosed the Indian "CTC Illusion" where employers bundle unvested paper equity, theoretical variable pay, and statutory retiral accruals into an inflated headline number. Proved that while contractual guaranteed cash (`guaranteed_cash_annual`, PT1) is reliably observable, non-guaranteed components suffer from extreme reporting ambiguity and zero longitudinal realization data. Formal dispositions established: (1) PT1 `guaranteed_cash_annual` strictly preserved as sole ML target powered by Rung F Level 2 Conformal CatBoost; (2) T8.02 (Variable Pay ML), T8.04 (Private Startup ESOP ML), and T8.07 (Offer ML) formally DEFERRED/REJECTED as stochastic ML targets; (3) T8.03 (Public RSUs) and T8.05 (Statutory Retirals) scoped strictly as deterministic, transparent algebraic calculators for Phase 9 UI/API; (4) T8.06 Scenario Engine v0 scoped as interactive user-driven parametric tool with strict zero-floor for illiquid startup equity. Documented findings in `docs/research/component-data-audit.md`. Completed and ratified all 14 questions of the Adversarial Review Checklist for Gate G7 (Section 4.4) and Gate G8 (Section 4.5). ## 13. How to update this file

1. After each task: add a session-log row, update section 3, and move `NEXT TASK` in `task.md`.
2. After each gate review: fill the gate log row with the decision, date and evidence.
3. Record any decision, assumption, bug or architectural change in its section on the day it happens.
4. Never rewrite history. Supersede a decision with a new row that references the old one.
5. If a rule was bent, cite its id and the reason in the session-log row (R-WF-07).
